void processDegreeInput(EditText degreeText, SeekArc seekArc) {\n        String degreeStr = degreeText.getText().toString().trim();\n        int degree;\n\n        if (degreeStr.isEmpty()) {\n            // Nothing was entered, simply restore the value the arc is set to.\n            degree = seekArc.getProgress();\n        } else {\n            try {\n                degree = Integer.parseInt(degreeStr);\n            } catch (NumberFormatException e) {\n                degreeText.setText(getResources().getString(R.string.zero));\n Here\'s the diagnosis and patch.\n\n## Root cause\n\nThe Robotic Arm servo controllers use a `SeekArc` whose progress range was the library default **0–100**, while the UI works in **0–360 degrees**. The activity therefore converted back and forth with a lossy scale factor of `3.6`, and papered over the resulting mismatch with an `editEnter` boolean flag:\n\n```java\n// onProgressChanged\nif (editEnter) { degreeText1.setText(String.valueOf(degree)); editEnter = false; }\nelse           { degreeText1.setText(String.valueOf((int) (i * 3.6))); }\n\n// processDegreeInput\nseekArc.setProgress((int) (degree / 3.6));   // fires the listener synchronously!\neditEnter = true;                            // set too late\n```\n\n`SeekArc.setProgress()` notifies the listener **synchronously**, so when the user typed `38`, the listener ran while `editEnter` was still `false` and overwrote the text with the round-tripped value `(int)(38/3.6) * 3.6 = (int)(10 * 3.6) = 36`. (The flag was then left dangling `true`, so the next drag would display a stale value too — a second symptom of the same hack.)\n\n## Fix\n\n1. **`servo_controller_layout.xml`** — give the arc the same resolution as the value range, so progress *is* the degree value and no rounding can occur:\n   ```xml\n   app:max="360"\n   ```\n   (verified in the built APK: `res-auto:max(0x7f0403ae)=360`)\n\n2. **`RoboticArmActivity.java`** — removed the `3.6` conversions and the `editEnter`/shared-`degree` flag hack:\n   - the four `onProgressChanged` callbacks simply display the progress value;\n   - `processDegreeInput()` uses a local `degree`, calls `setProgress(degree)` directly, and re-asserts the text afterwards (necessary because `setProgress()` doesn\'t notify when the value is unchanged);\n   - empty input now restores the arc\'s current value instead of relying on stale state;\n   - `0`/`360` bounds are now the constants `MIN_DEGREE`/`MAX_DEGREE`, also reused by the `InputMinMaxFilter`s.\n\n## Result for the reported steps\nDrag to `100` → arc progress `100`, text `100`. Type `38`, press Enter → `setProgress(38)`, text `38`. The displayed value now always equals the entered value, and the thumb position matches it exactly (previously it could only land on multiples of 3.6°).')], model='claude-opus-5', parent_tool_use_id=None, error=None, usage={'input_tokens': 2, 'cache_creation_input_tokens': 986, 'cache_read_input_tokens': 43861, 'cache_creation': {'ephemeral_5m_input_tokens': 986, 'ephemeral_1h_input_tokens': 0}, 'output_tokens': 1, 'service_tier': 'standard', 'inference_geo': 'global'}, message_id='msg_011CfHqw2u8AUWRpw9Hd9xsP', stop_reason=None, session_id='4488d23b-70ef-43d2-883a-ace32630b2d0', uuid='7acd56e7-5763-4171-9d81-132b3310b0a7')
